   101	The sentences of section 5.14 this task relies on are quoted here verbatim (C-88: the quoted sentences are normative; the path above is context, and the design file is uncommitted at authoring time):
   102	
   103	> 2. **無害化した環境を最初に** 最初の処理として `env -i` に入り、PATH は信頼ルートで解決した絶対バイナリへのリンクだけの私設ディレクトリ（shims、空、相対は拒否）。git は毎回 `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c protocol.allow=never -c protocol.https.allow=always` と明示 `-C`（https だけ許す。`protocol.allow=never` 単独は必要な fetch を止める）。 検査の読み取りは `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` で部分クローンの遅延 fetch（checkout 設定の credential.helper を起動しうる）を止め、`-c submodule.recurse=false --ignore-submodules=all` で submodule 内の status/diff（submodule 自身の設定が filter を有効化しうる）を止める。`.gitmodules` を持つ checkout は検査対象として拒否する。 `-C` は作業木を束縛しないので（repo-local の `core.worktree` が読み先を checkout 外へ向けられる）、git は `--git-dir=<checkout>/.git --work-tree=<checkout>` を明示し、`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` の realpath が checkout と一致しないときは拒否する。 premise や検査の「コマンド実行」は、固定語彙の読み取り専用プローブ（`git rev-parse <ref>`、`test -e <path>`、`grep -c <pattern> <file>` のように検査器自身が解釈する形）だけを許し、任意のシェル文字列は実行しない（本文のハッシュはコマンド文を認証するだけで、`python -I scripts/x.py` のファイル被演算子や `source ./x.sh` の中身は認証しない）。diff と show は `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` と `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c core.sshCommand=false` を付け、PR 側の `.gitattributes`（external diff、textconv、filter）が検査内容や実行物を変えないようにする。Python は `-I`。PATH 探索で実行するものは無い。 A shell script that runs under a PR-controlled or worker-controlled environment (verdict.sh, the chain glue, CI steps) is executed as a program with `#!/bin/bash -p`, never as `bash <file>`, and only by a trusted parent that supplies the clean environment before the exec: `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<passwd home> LANG=C.UTF-8 /bin/bash --noprofile --norc -p <absolute script path> …` (long options before `-p`). Privileged mode discards inherited functions, `BASH_ENV`/`ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, `CDPATH` and `GLOBIGNORE`, but on Linux an inherited `LD_PRELOAD`/`LD_AUDIT` already executes while the loader maps `/bin/bash`, before `-p` or any statement runs, so a self re-exec inside the script is not a boundary and re-executing `"$0"` would reopen the caller's pathname (a worker-owned symlink can be swapped between the two executions); the program therefore verifies and refuses instead of repairing: its environment must consist of exactly the allowed names (checked through absolute paths `/usr/bin/env`, `/usr/bin/grep`), `$0` resolved with `realpath` must be a regular file under the repository root, and HOME must equal the passwd home of the real uid (`/usr/bin/dscl . -read /Users/<user> NFSHomeDirectory` on macOS, `/usr/bin/getent passwd <uid>` on Linux), otherwise exit 2 with a named message. A caller-set sentinel variable is not a sanitization proof, because the caller controls it (ci-efficiency a04 and verdict a04 C-80 reviews, 2026-10-19). No script accepts a caller-supplied executable directory; interpreters are the fixed absolute paths item 9 names.
   104	
   105	> 4. **検証したものを実行し、検証した内容を渡す** 検証した実行体はその realpath で実行する（再探索しない）。検証したファイル内容は信頼ルート配下のスナップショット（0600、一意名）として渡し、使用直前に sha256 を再照合する。判断に読んだ証跡は sha256 を出力に記録する。
   106	
   107	> 5. **出力と予約の同一性** 名前には 40 桁の head を使う（7 桁で衝突させない）。lock は信頼ルート配下の固定 inode に O_EXCL で作り、置換を inode 比較で検出する。物理スロット 1 つに lock 1 つ。
   108	
   109	> 8. **承認はコミット済み台帳から読む** permission の決定行（`scope=permission approved_by=user diff_sha256=…`）は anchored checkout の作業コピーではなく、コミット済みの台帳から読む。読み方は `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` が返す commit sha を取り、GitHub API（`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/contents/.orchestration/decisions.tsv?ref=<sha>`、sandbox 内）でその sha の内容を取得する。ローカルの `git show` は共有 object database が書けるため中間 tree の差し替えで偽物を返しうる（第 3 回審査の所見 2）（ローカルの ref 名 `origin/main` は経由しない。`refs/replace/` も書けるので replacement を無効にする。Codex worker は `.git/objects` と `.git/refs` に書けるので ref は差し替えられるが、sha で指した object は内容アドレスで偽造できない）。作業コピーの行は提案で、記録 PR が main にマージされて初めて承認になる（PR の承認は操作者の操作）。偽造には main へのマージが要る。 The same applies to the PR content a mechanism evaluates (the verdict, the audit precheck, the counters): the changeset is read from GitHub at the 40-hex head (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/<base40>...<head40>`, `…/contents/<path>?ref=<head40>`) or from a private clone whose object store no worker can write; the main checkout's shared `.git/objects` and its `origin/main` ref are not evidence sources, because Codex workers write them (a04 C-80 round 1, 2026-10-19). Evidence files are named and matched by the full 40-hex head, never by a 7-character prefix. Every `gh` invocation names the repository explicitly (`--repo mryfmo/dotfiles` or the full pull-request URL): the local repository configuration selects the default repository otherwise, and a matching head sha does not bind the repository because a fork can hold the same commit (verdict a04 C-80 round 2, 2026-10-19). A mechanism whose application path runs code this contract does not yet cover (the agmsg storage layer sourced by `dispatch.sh`/`history.sh`; the integration gate until its own hardening task lands) names that task as the prerequisite of its `--apply` mode and runs shadow-only until the prerequisite is merged.
   110	
   111	> 9. **私設キャッシュ** orchestrator 側の実行は worker と共有するキャッシュ（`~/.cache/uv`、`~/.local/share/uv`、mise）から実行可能物を読まない。`UV_CACHE_DIR` を anchored checkout 配下（`.agents/worklog/uv-cache`、gitignored、worker 書き込み不可）に固定し、`uv run --no-config --no-project --offline --python <信頼ルートの絶対パスのインタプリタ>` で起動する（`--no-config` で uv の設定探索を止め、`--python` の絶対パスで cwd や親の `.venv` の発見を止める；敵対的な `.venv` と `uv.toml` を置いた fixture で検証）。hook は uv を使わず jq と python 標準ライブラリだけで動く。 One trusted interpreter resolver (`scripts/orchestrator/trusted-interpreter.sh`, P1-3 lineage) resolves `<abs>` from the repository's pinned declaration to a regular file under the tool manager's install root (the install root is anchored to the passwd home of the real uid, never to the HOME variable, which a caller can forge), refuses anything else, and every orchestrator-side script consumes it; no script keeps its own `command -v`, `mise which` or PATH fallback (ci-rerun.sh security review 44cb835, 2026-10-19). Shell code of other projects (the agmsg storage layer and its `drivers.partition` sourcing) is never sourced by an orchestrator-side mechanism; counters are read from the agmsg SQLite store read-only with a fixed query or from the pasted record.
   112	
   113	
   114	Host controls reduce accidents and prompt-injected damage. They do not isolate unrestricted processes sharing one UID. The design's GitHub prevention boundary is also not established while worker and orchestrator credentials remain accessible to that same UID; subsequent boundary-record checks provide detection as the design states.

   126	## Goal
   127	
   128	Produce one schema-valid verdict from nine positively checked conditions without executing inherited shell startup code, accepting caller-selected executable directories, following output symlinks or treating invalid `security` metadata as an exemption.
   129	
   130	Retain the a03 gate-before-application behavior, complete feedback validation, amended-cap precedence, offline operation, finding counts and trusted-checkout refresh. This task enables shadow mode only; `--apply` is prerequisite-gated as item 6 specifies.
   131	
   132	## Files
   133	
   134	1. `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh <pr> [--apply] [--loop] [--shadow]` remains Bash with `set -Eeuo pipefail`, English shdoc documentation and `jq` for JSON. Its startup and executable selection follow the Reset design below. Each condition is `{id, ok, evidence}` and becomes true only through a positive check of parsed, validated input.
   135	
   136	   Inputs remain the PR view and checks, the acceptance record's JSON block and prose, the selected task or `--task <id>`, the audit JSON, a fresh feedback sweep, saved feedback dispositions, the task's allowed paths and caps, regime counters, the GitHub merge-base changeset and the head-specific security review. The acceptance block names the PR, task and current head and contains a ledger array. The PR is open, non-draft and based on `main`. Read `head.sha` and `base.sha` from `gh api --repo mryfmo/dotfiles repos/mryfmo/dotfiles/pulls/<pr>`, require both to be 40 lowercase hexadecimal characters, and bind all reads to those values. This task chooses GitHub API reads, not a local clone: read the changeset through `…/compare/<base40>...<head40>` and inspected PR bytes through `…/contents/<path>?ref=<head40>`. Repository identity is fixed to `mryfmo/dotfiles` from the trusted repository declaration, not caller environment or local Git configuration. Every gh invocation, including instantiated API templates in the design quotation, supplies `--repo mryfmo/dotfiles`; API repository placeholders are fixed to `mryfmo/dotfiles`. A matching head SHA in a fork is not a repository binding. Missing, incomplete, truncated or unreadable API data holds; no local-head fetch or shared-object fallback is allowed.

The retained `PRECHECK_REQUIRED` setting is supplied explicitly as `--precheck-required 0|1`; absent or zero makes this condition's failure a warning. No environment exception is added for it.
   139	
   140	   Condition 2 requires every required check to be present with state `SUCCESS` for the head. Empty or unreadable checks hold. Every duplicate entry for a required name must also be `SUCCESS`.

A blocked audit holds. An incorrect audit without remaining findings fails this condition.

Consume the complete authenticated file list and its additions/deletions; account for both names of a rename. An unreadable merge base or incomplete changeset holds. The main checkout's `.git`, including `origin/main`, replacement refs and objects, is not an evidence source. Backticked exclusions after `outside` leave the outside count; default exclusions remain `tests/` and `.orchestration/`. Item 7 retains amendment precedence.

A sha7 filename is never a head match. Missing, null or non-boolean metadata holds with the named reason specified below.

Missing, invalid or different full-head metadata holds even when a filename or sha7 prefix matches. Hashes preserve consumed bytes; they do not authenticate a local Git object or ref.

Refusal before a usable task or head exists reports hold without inventing an output identity.

Do not use local tracking status, `origin/main` equality or shared objects to authenticate them. Execute verified snapshots as Reset design item 2 requires.    224	The required check names remain `validate`, `test (ubuntu-24.04, server)`, `test (ubuntu-24.04, client)`, `test (macos-14, client)`, `public-bootstrap (ubuntu-24.04, server)`, `public-bootstrap (ubuntu-24.04, client)` and `public-bootstrap (macos-14, client)`.
   225	
   226	## Reset design
   227	
   228	1. **A trusted parent supplies the clean environment before exec; startup verifies and refuses.**
   229	
   230	   `verdict.sh` has mode 0755 and shebang `#!/bin/bash -p`. A trusted parent invokes it with `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<passwd home> LANG=C.UTF-8 /bin/bash --noprofile --norc -p <absolute script path> …`, with long Bash options before `-p`. The parent supplies a clean environment before any exec, including the exec of `/usr/bin/env` itself; launching that prefix with inherited loader variables is not sanitization before the loader. Privileged mode discards inherited functions, `BASH_ENV`/`ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, `CDPATH` and `GLOBIGNORE`, but does not enable the loader’s secure-execution mode. On Linux, inherited `LD_PRELOAD` or `LD_AUDIT` can execute before Bash’s first statement. There is no self re-exec and no reopening of caller-controlled `$0`.
   231	
   232	   Before `set`, traps, argument processing or executable discovery, the first executable compound statement verifies privileged mode, the startup environment, the script path and HOME. A missing privileged mode refuses with exit 2 and `verdict: execute as a program`. Check environment names through absolute `/usr/bin/env` and `/usr/bin/grep`; exactly `PATH`, `HOME`, `LANG`, `PWD`, `SHLVL` and `_` are allowed, with fixed `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` and `LANG=C.UTF-8`. An extra/missing name or wrong fixed value refuses with exit 2 and `verdict: untrusted startup environment`. A caller-set sentinel proves nothing and is not accepted.
   233	
   234	   Resolve `$0` with the fixed absolute realpath utility and require an absolute invocation path whose realpath is a regular file under the anchored repository root; otherwise exit 2 with `verdict: untrusted script path`. Resolve the real uid through absolute `/usr/bin/id -ru` and obtain its passwd home through `/usr/bin/dscl . -read /Users/<user> NFSHomeDirectory` on macOS (the user is resolved from that real uid), or `/usr/bin/getent passwd <uid>` on Linux. Missing, ambiguous or unreadable account data, or HOME unequal to that passwd home, exits 2 with `verdict: HOME does not match passwd home`. The repository root and passwd-home lookup do not derive from caller HOME, PWD or local Git configuration. Refuse instead of repairing any failed check. No `compgen`, `unset -f` loop or PATH edit serves as the startup boundary.
   235	
   236	   Every ordinary caller, including the loop child and test helpers, is the trusted parent for that exec and uses the exact clean prefix with the passwd home and absolute script path; no sentinel is exported or trusted. The retained `PRECHECK_REQUIRED` control is passed as the explicit `--precheck-required 0|1` argument when needed, defaults to 0, and is never inherited or retained as an environment name.
   237	
   238	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_program_discards_hostile_startup_environment` executes the program with exported functions named `compgen`, `[`, `test`, `exec`, `command`, `builtin` and `command_not_found_handle`, a marker-writing `BASH_ENV`, `LD_PRELOAD` and `LD_AUDIT` naming marker-library paths, a forged HOME containing a marker-writing interpreter, and a planted PATH. The fixture also supplies the other startup selectors discarded by privileged mode. The direct dirty invocation must exit 2 with `verdict: untrusted startup environment` and no marker; it must not repair or re-exec. The direct dirty cases use deliberately non-loadable library paths: a loadable constructor cannot be prevented after a dirty exec. Exercise a loadable marker library only through the already-trusted parent supplying the clean environment before every exec, and require no marker. The clean prefix must yield the unchanged clean verdict and conditions, so an incidental exit 2 cannot pass the positive control.
   239	
   240	   Separate subcases exercise each named refusal: a forged HOME with otherwise allowed names and fixed values, a symlinked `$0` resolving outside the repository, and `VERDICT_SANITIZED=1` together with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_INDEX_FILE` pointing to planted files. Require the appropriate named message, exit 2, no marker or application effect and no consumption of planted configuration, objects or index. Check the clean child’s allowed-name-only environment and passwd-derived HOME. Exercise the exact long-options-before-`-p` command.
   241	
   242	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_refuses_bash_file_invocation` invokes `bash scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh …` in a clean fixture and requires exit 2 and the named message. This negative invocation is the sole deliberate plain non-privileged Bash verdict call in the tests.
   243	
   244	2. **Executable identities are fixed at the trusted entrypoint.**
   245	
   246	   No caller-supplied executable directory exists. Remove `VERDICT_BIN`, its environment whitelist, symlink-directory acceptance and every equivalent caller override from the script and fixtures. Do not replace them with a differently named environment variable or CLI option.
   247	
   248	   The trusted entrypoint fixes the absolute paths for Bash, uv, Python, gh, git, jq and required utilities before reading PR-controlled input. It executes verified realpaths without rediscovery. The repository's Python declaration is a read-only input; consume `scripts/orchestrator/trusted-interpreter.sh` from the authenticated trusted base when present. The P1-3 resolver alone resolves the pinned declaration to a regular executable file under the tool manager's install root, anchored to the passwd home of the real uid, never to HOME, and outside worktrees, worker-writable caches and `$TMPDIR`. A symlink spelling alone authenticates nothing. An absent resolver or invalid trusted installation holds rather than selecting another interpreter from PATH, mise, uv discovery or a checkout. No private resolver, `command -v` or `mise which` fallback is retained here. Implementation may proceed before `dotfiles-T133-ci-efficiency-followup-a04` lands by implementing and testing this unavailable-resolver hold with a trusted resolver fixture for successful cases; live execution stays on hold until that lineage supplies the resolver. This task therefore does not need the resolver at implementation time and adds no P1-3 `after:` dependency.
   249	
   250	   Design 5.14 items 2, 4 and 9 apply by reference to Git invocation, executable paths, verified snapshots and private cache use. The uv invocation is `uv run --no-config --no-project --offline --python <abs>`, with uv itself an internally fixed absolute executable and `<abs>` the validated interpreter realpath. `UV_CACHE_DIR` is the anchored private cache from the design. Worker-shared uv or mise caches supply no executable. Python children use `-I`; the a03 `-B`, `sys.dont_write_bytecode` and refusal of checkout bytecode that helper imports could consume remain enforced.
   251	
   252	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_uses_only_declared_absolute_executables` verifies fixed tool identities, refusal of hostile PATH and inherited interpreter selectors, and unchanged clean-prefix execution, including an absolute symlink to a marker-writing interpreter and a forged HOME install tree that cannot become the trusted install root.
   253	
   254	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_rejects_interpreters_in_worktrees_caches_or_tmp` covers each excluded root, a non-regular target, a forged-HOME install root and a valid regular-file control under the passwd-home-anchored install root.
   255	
   256	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_executes_verified_snapshots_and_records_input_hashes` changes a validated source or input before consumption and verifies refusal or consumption of the verified snapshot, never the substituted contents, and checks the recorded input hashes.
   257	
   258	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_holds_without_the_shared_trusted_interpreter_resolver` verifies the interim hold and absence of interpreter discovery or application effects.
   259	
   260	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_reads_pasted_counters_without_sourcing_agmsg` plants a marker-writing agmsg driver and counter executable, supplies the valid pasted counters, and requires unchanged verdicts with no marker; missing, duplicate, mismatched and unreadable counter cases hold.
   261	
   262	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_ignores_replaced_local_objects_and_refs` serves forged PR bytes through a replaced local object and forged `origin/main`, while fixed GitHub fixtures return the authentic full-head changeset and contents. It requires the clean verdict and authenticated input hashes, no local-object evidence read, and hold on incomplete API data rather than a local fallback. Cover precheck ancestry, fix-commit membership, application equality and loop refresh through the same GitHub fixtures.
   263	
   264	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_names_the_repository_on_every_gh_call` runs with a fixed fake gh that records every argument vector and asserts `--repo mryfmo/dotfiles` on every call, covering PR view/checks, API and feedback reads, prerequisite-ledger reads, loop refresh and fixture-only merge. A fork fixture containing the same head SHA cannot redirect a call. The application subcase supplies both authenticated prerequisite merges and uses only fake effects.
   265	
   266	   The retained `test_verdict_pins_tools_interpreter_and_environment_before_reading_a_pr_checkout` and `test_verdict_never_imports_cached_bytecode` remain green. Unavailable offline PyYAML still holds with no online fallback.
   267	
   268	3. **The Python child contains persistent writes.**
   269	
   270	   Verdict JSON and shadow logging are opened through the fixed isolated Python child inside `<repo>/.orchestration/validation/`. Their resolved paths remain inside the repository and the validation directory. Symlinks, non-regular files and paths outside that boundary refuse before truncation or append. Directory-component validation and descriptor checks prevent a symlinked parent from defeating a leaf-only check.
   271	
   272	   Both opens use `O_NOFOLLOW`; shadow logging also uses `O_APPEND`. Regular-file checks apply to the opened descriptor. Shell `>` and `>>` redirections do not open these destinations. The JSON is schema-valid before it is committed to its destination. A write refusal holds and performs neither dispatch nor merge.
   273	
   274	   On `hold`, the shadow log receives nothing, whether absent or already populated. A contained hold JSON may still be written when task and head are known.
   275	
   276	   New verdict filenames use the full head; every shadow row retains the full head. No reservation or lock mechanism is introduced.
   277	
   278	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_opens_outputs_without_following_symlinks` plants a symlink separately at the JSON destination and shadow-log destination, requires refusal, and verifies each target's bytes remain unchanged. It includes escaping-parent and non-regular destination controls.
   279	
   280	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_shadow_does_not_append_on_hold` checks absent and populated logs and unchanged prior bytes. The existing successful shadow-column test remains green.
   281	
   282	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_output_names_use_the_full_head` checks the full-head JSON filename and shadow identity rather than accepting a seven-character output name.
   283	
   284	4. **Security metadata is an explicit boolean.**
   285	
   286	   Validate the parsed task's `security` value before selecting condition 8's branch. Missing, null or non-boolean values produce condition 8 failure, overall hold with exit 20, and the named reason `security must be an explicit boolean`. They never produce `not a security task`, append a shadow row, dispatch or merge.
   287	
   288	   Only boolean `false` permits the no-security-review branch. Boolean `true` retains the required head-specific review, with front-matter `head:` exactly equal to the verdict's full 40-hex head. A quoted `"false"` is a string and does not exempt the task.
   289	
   290	   `tests/unit/test_verdict.py::VerdictTest::test_verdict_rejects_evidence_for_another_full_head_with_the_same_sha7` uses two distinct 40-hex heads sharing their first seven characters. Separate subcases substitute the audit, security report, local review and saved or fresh sweep; each mismatched or absent full-head field holds despite a matching prefix and a correct security verdict. Full-head-matched controls remain green.

A verified gate-script snapshot does not bind descendant execution or live reads, and shadow mode does not treat gate conclusions as binding."
  - control: task metadata

    disposition: "residual: a trusted parent supplies the clean environment before any exec, startup checks refuse rather than repair, and internally fixed executable paths remove the caller-bin interface; shared-UID access to installations, credentials and host state remains outside the isolation these controls provide."

  - control: "design 5.14 item 3 (trusted path boundaries)"

  - control: "design 5.14 item 5 (output and reservation identity)"

  - control: "design 5.14 item 6 (honest residuals)"
    disposition: "residual: startup, path and snapshot controls are reported as damage reduction; neither host isolation nor independent GitHub credentials is claimed."
  - control: "design 5.14 item 7 (verification)"
    disposition: "residual: this task supplies concrete regression selectors and final-head evidence; the shared trusted-execution test helper remains owned by P1-6 and is reused only if available without expanding this task's edit boundary."
  - control: "design 5.14 item 8 (committed approval ledger)"
    disposition: "residual: verdict evaluation supplies no permission approval; every gh call names mryfmo/dotfiles explicitly, and PR data and prerequisite-merge ledger records come from GitHub at full SHAs, never shared refs or objects. Shadow mode binds no gate conclusion; application awaits both merged hardening prerequisites, and shared credentials remain a residual."
  - control: "design 5.14 item 9 (private cache and absolute interpreter)"
    disposition: "residual: the shared trusted-interpreter resolver anchors the install root to the passwd home of the real uid, never HOME, and the anchored private uv cache excludes worktrees, worker-writable caches and temporary directories; until the P1-3 resolver is available, verdict holds without a private resolver or PATH fallback."
  - control: "design 5.14 item 10 (worker commands remain sandboxed)"

# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a04 — INV-6 S1 re-plan of a03 (plan 024 P1-4a; PR #410)

Worker: a008, Claude Code, worktree `worker-f`.

This text is the independent redesign by a review-profile context other than the author of the predecessor task texts. The a03 pre-push fix round and revise 1 exhausted its C-61 allowance. The security review of `513e5d05` found caller-controlled executable selection and cached-bytecode execution; the review of `6b7e7833` found inherited `compgen` execution, acceptance of attacker-selected absolute symlink targets, uncontained output writes and an implicit non-security branch.

The a03 acceptance record contains three `env-trust-bypass` rows: `security:513e5d0:1`, `security:6b7e783:1` and `security:6b7e783:2`. Its recorded `re-plan:` line establishes INV-6 S1. Another a03 patch round is not authorized. This reset changes the execution contract so a trusted parent supplies the clean environment before exec, the program verifies and refuses without self re-exec, and no caller executable directory exists.

Read these records before implementation:

- `.orchestration/tasks/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a03.md`
- `.orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a03.md`
- `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a03-security-513e5d0.md.last.md`
- `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a03-security-6b7e783.md.last.md`

The design at `$HOME/Workspace/dotfiles/.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md`, section 5.14, applies by reference, including today's amendments to items 2, 8 and 9 and items 4 and 5. This absolute path identifies the orchestrator's uncommitted working copy under C-86. The task does not claim that section is already on `origin/main`.

The sentences of section 5.14 this task relies on are quoted here verbatim (C-88: the quoted sentences are normative; the path above is context, and the design file is uncommitted at authoring time):

> 2. **無害化した環境を最初に** 最初の処理として `env -i` に入り、PATH は信頼ルートで解決した絶対バイナリへのリンクだけの私設ディレクトリ（shims、空、相対は拒否）。git は毎回 `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c protocol.allow=never -c protocol.https.allow=always` と明示 `-C`（https だけ許す。`protocol.allow=never` 単独は必要な fetch を止める）。 検査の読み取りは `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` で部分クローンの遅延 fetch（checkout 設定の credential.helper を起動しうる）を止め、`-c submodule.recurse=false --ignore-submodules=all` で submodule 内の status/diff（submodule 自身の設定が filter を有効化しうる）を止める。`.gitmodules` を持つ checkout は検査対象として拒否する。 `-C` は作業木を束縛しないので（repo-local の `core.worktree` が読み先を checkout 外へ向けられる）、git は `--git-dir=<checkout>/.git --work-tree=<checkout>` を明示し、`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` の realpath が checkout と一致しないときは拒否する。 premise や検査の「コマンド実行」は、固定語彙の読み取り専用プローブ（`git rev-parse <ref>`、`test -e <path>`、`grep -c <pattern> <file>` のように検査器自身が解釈する形）だけを許し、任意のシェル文字列は実行しない（本文のハッシュはコマンド文を認証するだけで、`python -I scripts/x.py` のファイル被演算子や `source ./x.sh` の中身は認証しない）。diff と show は `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` と `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c core.sshCommand=false` を付け、PR 側の `.gitattributes`（external diff、textconv、filter）が検査内容や実行物を変えないようにする。Python は `-I`。PATH 探索で実行するものは無い。 A shell script that runs under a PR-controlled or worker-controlled environment (verdict.sh, the chain glue, CI steps) is executed as a program with `#!/bin/bash -p`, never as `bash <file>`, and only by a trusted parent that supplies the clean environment before the exec: `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<passwd home> LANG=C.UTF-8 /bin/bash --noprofile --norc -p <absolute script path> …` (long options before `-p`). Privileged mode discards inherited functions, `BASH_ENV`/`ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, `CDPATH` and `GLOBIGNORE`, but on Linux an inherited `LD_PRELOAD`/`LD_AUDIT` already executes while the loader maps `/bin/bash`, before `-p` or any statement runs, so a self re-exec inside the script is not a boundary and re-executing `"$0"` would reopen the caller's pathname (a worker-owned symlink can be swapped between the two executions); the program therefore verifies and refuses instead of repairing: its environment must consist of exactly the allowed names (checked through absolute paths `/usr/bin/env`, `/usr/bin/grep`), `$0` resolved with `realpath` must be a regular file under the repository root, and HOME must equal the passwd home of the real uid (`/usr/bin/dscl . -read /Users/<user> NFSHomeDirectory` on macOS, `/usr/bin/getent passwd <uid>` on Linux), otherwise exit 2 with a named message. A caller-set sentinel variable is not a sanitization proof, because the caller controls it (ci-efficiency a04 and verdict a04 C-80 reviews, 2026-10-19). No script accepts a caller-supplied executable directory; interpreters are the fixed absolute paths item 9 names.

> 4. **検証したものを実行し、検証した内容を渡す** 検証した実行体はその realpath で実行する（再探索しない）。検証したファイル内容は信頼ルート配下のスナップショット（0600、一意名）として渡し、使用直前に sha256 を再照合する。判断に読んだ証跡は sha256 を出力に記録する。

> 5. **出力と予約の同一性** 名前には 40 桁の head を使う（7 桁で衝突させない）。lock は信頼ルート配下の固定 inode に O_EXCL で作り、置換を inode 比較で検出する。物理スロット 1 つに lock 1 つ。

> 8. **承認はコミット済み台帳から読む** permission の決定行（`scope=permission approved_by=user diff_sha256=…`）は anchored checkout の作業コピーではなく、コミット済みの台帳から読む。読み方は `git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main` が返す commit sha を取り、GitHub API（`gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/contents/.orchestration/decisions.tsv?ref=<sha>`、sandbox 内）でその sha の内容を取得する。ローカルの `git show` は共有 object database が書けるため中間 tree の差し替えで偽物を返しうる（第 3 回審査の所見 2）（ローカルの ref 名 `origin/main` は経由しない。`refs/replace/` も書けるので replacement を無効にする。Codex worker は `.git/objects` と `.git/refs` に書けるので ref は差し替えられるが、sha で指した object は内容アドレスで偽造できない）。作業コピーの行は提案で、記録 PR が main にマージされて初めて承認になる（PR の承認は操作者の操作）。偽造には main へのマージが要る。 The same applies to the PR content a mechanism evaluates (the verdict, the audit precheck, the counters): the changeset is read from GitHub at the 40-hex head (`gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/<base40>...<head40>`, `…/contents/<path>?ref=<head40>`) or from a private clone whose object store no worker can write; the main checkout's shared `.git/objects` and its `origin/main` ref are not evidence sources, because Codex workers write them (a04 C-80 round 1, 2026-10-19). Evidence files are named and matched by the full 40-hex head, never by a 7-character prefix. Every `gh` invocation names the repository explicitly (`--repo mryfmo/dotfiles` or the full pull-request URL): the local repository configuration selects the default repository otherwise, and a matching head sha does not bind the repository because a fork can hold the same commit (verdict a04 C-80 round 2, 2026-10-19). A mechanism whose application path runs code this contract does not yet cover (the agmsg storage layer sourced by `dispatch.sh`/`history.sh`; the integration gate until its own hardening task lands) names that task as the prerequisite of its `--apply` mode and runs shadow-only until the prerequisite is merged.

> 9. **私設キャッシュ** orchestrator 側の実行は worker と共有するキャッシュ（`~/.cache/uv`、`~/.local/share/uv`、mise）から実行可能物を読まない。`UV_CACHE_DIR` を anchored checkout 配下（`.agents/worklog/uv-cache`、gitignored、worker 書き込み不可）に固定し、`uv run --no-config --no-project --offline --python <信頼ルートの絶対パスのインタプリタ>` で起動する（`--no-config` で uv の設定探索を止め、`--python` の絶対パスで cwd や親の `.venv` の発見を止める；敵対的な `.venv` と `uv.toml` を置いた fixture で検証）。hook は uv を使わず jq と python 標準ライブラリだけで動く。 One trusted interpreter resolver (`scripts/orchestrator/trusted-interpreter.sh`, P1-3 lineage) resolves `<abs>` from the repository's pinned declaration to a regular file under the tool manager's install root (the install root is anchored to the passwd home of the real uid, never to the HOME variable, which a caller can forge), refuses anything else, and every orchestrator-side script consumes it; no script keeps its own `command -v`, `mise which` or PATH fallback (ci-rerun.sh security review 44cb835, 2026-10-19). Shell code of other projects (the agmsg storage layer and its `drivers.partition` sourcing) is never sourced by an orchestrator-side mechanism; counters are read from the agmsg SQLite store read-only with a fixed query or from the pasted record.


Host controls reduce accidents and prompt-injected damage. They do not isolate unrestricted processes sharing one UID. The design's GitHub prevention boundary is also not established while worker and orchestrator credentials remain accessible to that same UID; subsequent boundary-record checks provide detection as the design states.

## Branch and reset boundary

Continue `tools/verdict-audit-pool-a01`, PR #410, from `6b7e7833a85528440dc397c2555bc9dd603c685a`, already update-branched onto main `3f240b12`. Keep the a03 implementation, regression coverage and evidence. Create neither another implementation branch nor another PR.

The first push deletes `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a03/task.md` and adds the dispatched text byte-for-byte at `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a04/task.md`. This single predecessor-copy deletion is the explicit reset-bookkeeping exception to `allowed_files`. Other a03 evidence remains unchanged under its original task identifier and tested heads.

The orchestrator retains the a03 acceptance history, class rows, stopped status and counters. The final inventory identifies preserved a03 evidence separately from a04 additions; preservation does not authorize further worker edits in the a03 directory. The a01/a02 artifact removals already completed by a03 are not repeated or reversed.

The split remains intact. The pool sibling owns `scripts/orchestrator/audit-pool.sh`, changes to `scripts/orchestrator/orch-audit.sh`, `.schemas/classes.yaml` and `tests/unit/test_audit_pool.py`. `audit-pool.sh` is absent at `6b7e7833`; the audit schema at that head is `.schemas/audit.json`. Neither fact authorizes recreating a split file or adding a `schemas/` mirror. PR #407 remains parked; its parking operation is not repeated.

## Goal

Produce one schema-valid verdict from nine positively checked conditions without executing inherited shell startup code, accepting caller-selected executable directories, following output symlinks or treating invalid `security` metadata as an exemption.

Retain the a03 gate-before-application behavior, complete feedback validation, amended-cap precedence, offline operation, finding counts and trusted-checkout refresh. This task enables shadow mode only; `--apply` is prerequisite-gated as item 6 specifies.

## Files

1. `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh <pr> [--apply] [--loop] [--shadow]` remains Bash with `set -Eeuo pipefail`, English shdoc documentation and `jq` for JSON. Its startup and executable selection follow the Reset design below. Each condition is `{id, ok, evidence}` and becomes true only through a positive check of parsed, validated input.

   Inputs remain the PR view and checks, the acceptance record's JSON block and prose, the selected task or `--task <id>`, the audit JSON, a fresh feedback sweep, saved feedback dispositions, the task's allowed paths and caps, regime counters, the GitHub merge-base changeset and the head-specific security review. The acceptance block names the PR, task and current head and contains a ledger array. The PR is open, non-draft and based on `main`. Read `head.sha` and `base.sha` from `gh api --repo mryfmo/dotfiles repos/mryfmo/dotfiles/pulls/<pr>`, require both to be 40 lowercase hexadecimal characters, and bind all reads to those values. This task chooses GitHub API reads, not a local clone: read the changeset through `…/compare/<base40>...<head40>` and inspected PR bytes through `…/contents/<path>?ref=<head40>`. Repository identity is fixed to `mryfmo/dotfiles` from the trusted repository declaration, not caller environment or local Git configuration. Every gh invocation, including instantiated API templates in the design quotation, supplies `--repo mryfmo/dotfiles`; API repository placeholders are fixed to `mryfmo/dotfiles`. A matching head SHA in a fork is not a repository binding. Missing, incomplete, truncated or unreadable API data holds; no local-head fetch or shared-object fallback is allowed.

   Condition 1 verifies that the GitHub contents response for `.orchestration/<task>/precheck.json?ref=<head40>` hashes to the record's `precheck:` value and names the head or an ancestor authenticated by GitHub compare data whose diff to the head contains only `.orchestration/<task>/` paths. The retained `PRECHECK_REQUIRED` setting is supplied explicitly as `--precheck-required 0|1`; absent or zero makes this condition's failure a warning. No environment exception is added for it.

   Condition 2 requires every required check to be present with state `SUCCESS` for the head. Empty or unreadable checks hold. Every duplicate entry for a required name must also be `SUCCESS`.

   Condition 3 requires an audit that validates against `.schemas/audit.json`, names the task and full head, has zero open P0/P1 findings, and has verdict `correct` or a non-empty set of remaining findings whose scopes are all `records`. A blocked audit holds. An incorrect audit without remaining findings fails this condition.

   Condition 4 requires saved and fresh feedback documents to name the accepted head, complete feedback identity matching with multiplicity, and a valid disposition for every fresh item. Diagnostic URLs do not determine the result.

   Condition 5 requires every path in the base-to-head diff to match an `allowed_files` entry and additions-plus-deletions totals to remain within the effective Check caps. The comparison is `gh api --repo mryfmo/dotfiles repos/mryfmo/dotfiles/compare/<base40>...<head40>` using the PR API's base and head, and its full `merge_base_commit.sha` identifies the merge base. Consume the complete authenticated file list and its additions/deletions; account for both names of a rename. An unreadable merge base or incomplete changeset holds. The main checkout's `.git`, including `origin/main`, replacement refs and objects, is not an evidence source. Backticked exclusions after `outside` leave the outside count; default exclusions remain `tests/` and `.orchestration/`. Item 7 retains amendment precedence.

   Condition 6 reads one orchestrator-pasted INV-6 counters block in the acceptance record, bound there to this task, PR and full head. This task chooses the pasted record rather than a live store query so verdict executes no agmsg shell code and cannot follow worker-writable `drivers.partition`. It never invokes `scripts/regime-counters.sh` or sources the agmsg storage layer. No `S1:` line is present and the parsed decimal `revises` value is below 2. A missing ledger or counters block, duplicate block, mismatched binding or unreadable count holds; an absent count is never zero.

   Condition 7 requires the numeric count of open P0/P1 findings to be zero. Findings with status `new`, `still-open` or absent count as open regardless of identifier or scope.

   Condition 8 first validates `security` as an explicit boolean. Only boolean `false` permits the non-security branch. Boolean `true` requires the security review's front-matter `head:` to equal the verdict's own full 40-hex head and its last non-blank line to be `Verdict: correct`. A sha7 filename is never a head match. Missing, null or non-boolean metadata holds with the named reason specified below.

   Condition 9 requires at least one `sha256:<hex> <path>` line in the acceptance record's prose and verifies every such line against its file. Every inspected evidence file, including audit, security, local review and saved or fresh sweep, is named with and matched against the verdict's full 40-hex head: Markdown uses front-matter `head:`, audit and local-review JSON use `head` (on every record for a JSON list), and feedback JSON uses `head_sha`. Missing, invalid or different full-head metadata holds even when a filename or sha7 prefix matches. Hashes preserve consumed bytes; they do not authenticate a local Git object or ref.

   All nine conditions passing produces `accept`. Failure of condition 6 or 7, missing or unreadable input, schema-invalid input, invalid security metadata or an internal evaluation error produces `hold`. Other failed conditions produce `revise`. The revise line remains `AGMSG-ACCEPTANCE v1 task_id=<task> status=revise previous_head=<head> reason=<failed ids> next_action=<the first failed condition's evidence, shell-safe>`. The orchestrator supplies `check=` selectors.

   The generated verdict is `.orchestration/validation/<task>-verdict-<head>.json`, using the full 40-character head under design 5.14 item 5. Its fields remain `verdict`, `pr`, `head`, `task_id`, `conditions[]`, `inputs_sha256{}`, `generated_at` and optional `revise_line`, validated against `.schemas/verdict.json`. Audit, security, local-review and sweep inputs use full-head-named verified snapshots with the metadata bindings above. External producers are not edited; the orchestrator supplies the full-head-named evidence and required metadata before evaluation, retaining original artifacts separately. Historical verdict files remain untouched.

   Exit codes remain 0 for accept, 10 for revise, 20 for hold and 2 for usage or internal error, with internal error treated as hold. A destination or post-startup execution-boundary refusal holds with exit 20; the mandatory startup refusals in Reset design item 1 exit 2 with their named messages. Refusal before a usable task or head exists reports hold without inventing an output identity.

   `--apply` first requires both authenticated prerequisite-merge records in item 6, then requires the trusted execution context and scripts, schemas and task files whose consumed bytes and complete file inventory equal GitHub's `base40` tree, read through its API at that SHA, with no substituted or extra executable helper in those code directories. Do not use local tracking status, `origin/main` equality or shared objects to authenticate them. Execute verified snapshots as Reset design item 2 requires. The last four shadow rows must agree between machine and human and represent four distinct PRs. Four rows for one PR do not unlock application. Item 6 retains the integration gate before either application effect.

   After both prerequisite merges, accept and a successful hardened gate, application writes the accepted line to a temporary file, sends it through `scripts/orchestrator/dispatch.sh <team> <from> <to> <pane> <file> <record>`, then invokes `gh pr merge <pr> --repo mryfmo/dotfiles --squash --match-head-commit <head>`. Its reason names `verdict-<head>`. Team, sender, recipient and placement remain explicit arguments. Arguments containing `--admin` or `--auto` anywhere are refused with exit 2 before input reads or tool calls other than mandatory startup verification.

17:## Regime activation and progress
21:- A Claude session started in the repository root outside Herdr (a plain shell, mosh/ssh, or `claude -p`) is a pane-less orchestrator: nothing seats a worker automatically, and the SessionStart `herdr-agents --attach` hook prints a summary line naming that state, the on-demand commands, and any seated worker, followed in a regime repository by the `agmsg-orchestration:` directive line. Bring the regime up on demand: claim the seat outside the sandbox with the composite instance id, `actas-claim.sh <repo> claude-code <name> <session_id>.<claude pid>` (a claim from sandboxed Bash writes the bare session id and turn delivery then skips silently; see the seat-lock bullet below); seat the worker with `herdr-agents --add-worker <worktree>` (it derives `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH` from the default Herdr server socket `~/.config/herdr/herdr.sock`, the path the Claude sandbox allowlists, before creating anything, accepts a claude worker's workspace-trust dialog during spawn's readiness wait, and takes `--ready-timeout <seconds>`); confirm the worker's placement from its `run/spawn.*` placement record itself (the `herdr:<socket>:<pane>` row that upstream `agmsg_spawn_path` resolves) and the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints, never from `team.sh <team> --json`, which observes Codex members by reading their pane; treat the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints as the `AGMSG-PING` evidence, and on `pong=no` or `linkage=unreached` wake again with `agmsg-dispatch <team> <orchestrator> <worker> <pane> 'AGMSG-PING v1 task_id=<id> reason=<reason>'` and verify the PING's `read_at` (`poke.sh` only for a viewed workspace); and dispatch no AGMSG-TASK before its `AGMSG-PONG` arrives. Without a managed workspace the auditor runs headless, in the form the task-level audit bullet below names. A pane-less session uses turn delivery when the Monitor cannot run; see "Identity, delivery, and storage" for the platform limits.
29:## Parallel workers
33:- Delegate all repository-mutating work — file edits, builds, test runs, and git state changes — to seated workers, with at most one seated worker per git worktree and sequential assignments within one worktree. Add worktrees for parallelism; never use parallel `codex exec` or per-task Codex spawning, except that the read-only, non-interactive task-level audit the orchestrator runs during acceptance (the task-level audit bullet below) is not worker spawning and is permitted; it is identity-less. agmsg/herdr control-plane commands (`delivery.sh`, `watch.sh`, `actas-claim.sh`, `send.sh`, `join.sh`, and herdr agent/pane commands) are orchestrator-side exemptions.
43:  - Some steps stay sequential. The single audit tab serializes audits; the task-level audit of T67 reduces them to one per task. The orchestrator-side gate (`make require-crit-review`) and merges also run one at a time.
46:## Identity, delivery, and storage
59:## Live verification
64:## Review and integration invariants
71:- A task that touches a trust boundary (anything the design tier lists, CI that decides a required check, code that executes PR-controlled input) carries a `threat_table` in its front matter: one `{control, disposition}` row per thing the PR or the orchestrator controls (the tree and its tests, task.md, workflow files, interpreter and environment files, in-process effects, new or renamed scripts, metadata separators). The row unit is one row per `allowed_files` entry, one row per listed control that is not a path (task metadata, workflow and interpreter or environment inputs, in-process effects, new or renamed scripts, metadata separators), carried by the declared test module's row where that is where the control acts, and one row per orchestrator effect from the fixed list (forced removal, merge, update-branch); under a wildcard `allowed_files` entry whose files are executed or applied (scripts, workflows, hooks, settings, the rules seats follow), one row per concrete file the PR changes; a wildcard over test fixtures or other data files keeps one row; C4 and C7b of `scripts/orchestrator/task-consistency-check.py` verify the path half before dispatch, and the task-file review checks the rest. A disposition takes one of three forms: `closed: tests/<file>::<name>`, the test that closes the row; `residual: <sentence>`, the compensating control; `out-of-scope: <task id>`, the task that owns the row. The task-file review checks the three shape rules by hand: a `closed` selector names a test present in the PR tree at acceptance, an `out-of-scope` owner is a task id in the design's `implementing_tasks`, a `residual` payload is a sentence of at least 20 characters (the gate's minimum for a reason). For a `closed` row it checks the selector's form only, since the test does not exist before implementation. Substance is the reviewers': the task-file review, the Bot and the auditor judge each row against the diff. The table is the task's completion condition: a missing row is added once, counted as an amendment, never fixed finding by finding.
72:- Before every acceptance action (revise, accept, merge) the orchestrator runs `scripts/regime-counters.sh <task id>` and pastes its output into the acceptance record. S2 is decided from INV-6's counts: each component the script prints is read from its output, and for each component it prints as `unknown` the record pastes the command and output that read the source INV-6 names for that component, or, while that source has no entry for the task, says so and pastes the reading of the interim record the orchestrator used in its place. S1 (plan 006: the same finding class three times on one artifact, from any reviewer) is decided from the record's finding-class table, which gives a class to every finding the PR-feedback sweep lists, whatever its disposition, and to every task-level audit finding; three rows of one class stop the artifact. Read that table over non-advisory rows only: list advisory P2 rows with their classes (`advisory-p2` and the `local:<sha7>:<n>` rows `local-advisory.sh` writes), exclude them from S1's three-rows count, and name the excluded rows in the record. The gate's S6 check still sees every ledger row, so an advisory class at three rows gets its `searches` entry from `acceptance-front-matter.py` like any other. INV-6's message and head counts and the S2 lines (`revises`, `amendments`, `questions`, `bot_p1_heads`) take no ledger rows; `scripts/regime-counters.sh` prints them unchanged.
95:## Message Contract v1
144:## `.orchestration` Workspace Layout
157:## Orchestrator Playbook
170:       - A P2 finding of the Codex Bot, local review or task-level audit is advisory: disposition it `not-applicable:advisory P2 owned by <lineage>-followup-a0<n>` and record an `<id><TAB><class>` row under `## Owned by <owner>` in the lineage's acceptance record, with the orchestrator writing the owner task file before the row.
173:    2. Run the task-level audit of that head in the pair or headless form of the task-level audit bullet. It exits nonzero for every verdict other than `correct`. An exit with `Audit verdict: incorrect` is not a failed step: continue to step 3 and disposition its findings. A `blocked` or missing verdict means re-running the audit.
174:    3. Write the acceptance record, summarising the dispositions, with every audit finding dispositioned as the task-level audit bullet says: for an `incorrect` verdict the record's JSON `audit_findings`, which the gate reads through `ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE`.

 succeeded in 0ms:
---
name: ponytail
description: >
  Lazy senior dev mode: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a
  reply a busy human understands in one read. Use on any coding task (writing,
  fixing, refactoring, reviewing, choosing dependencies) and when the user says
  "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest solution", "yagni", or complains about
  over-engineering or bloat. Levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
argument-hint: "[lite|full|ultra|off]"
license: MIT
---

# Ponytail

You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.

Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.

## Before you write

Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.

## The smallest complete change

Take the first option that fully works:

1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.

- Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
- No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
- The shortest working diff wins, once you know everything it must touch. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
- Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
- Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
- Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
- Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
- Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
- A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.

Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.

## Levels

| Level | Behavior |
|-------|----------|
| **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
| **full** | The rules above. Default. |
| **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |

## Codex seat worklogs

Project layouts vary by language. Set up this worklog structure only when it
does not already exist, and use timestamped filenames in `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS`
form:

- `.agents/worklog/codex/plan/<timestamp>_plan.md` stores the plan and design
  written before implementation. Ask the user questions when needed, and
  update the plan when questions, learning, or completed tasks change it. It
  must contain `Goal`, `Scope`, `Assumptions`, `Design`, `Tests`, and
  `Open Questions`.
- `.agents/worklog/codex/todo/<timestamp>_todo.md` derives its tasks from the
  plan. Move completed items from `TODO` to `Done`; when `TODO` is empty, set
  its status to `done` and rename it to `<timestamp>_done.md`. It must contain
  `TODO` and `Done`.
- `.agents/worklog/codex/learn/<timestamp>_learn.md` records only reusable,
  validated knowledge that speeds a future decision. State what was learned
  and where it applies, update the plan's `Assumptions`, `Design`, or `Tests`
  when relevant. A learn file must contain `Date`, `Learnings`, and
  `Plan Updates`.

Every plan, todo, and learn file starts with YAML frontmatter containing
`type` (`plan`, `todo`, or `learn`), `id` (`YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS`), `owner` (for
example, `codex-a`), and ISO8601 `created_at` and `updated_at`. Additionally:

- todo requires `status`, `workstream`, and `related_plan`; status is one of
  `active`, `blocked`, `done`, or `superseded`;
- plan requires `status`, one of `draft`, `active`, `done`, or `superseded`;
- learn requires `validated` (`true` or `false`) and `apply_to` (plan/tests),
  and may be created only when reusable and validated.

Optional frontmatter keys are `depends_on` (todo ID array), `blocked_reason`
for blocked work, `evidence` (path array), and `tags`.

 succeeded in 106ms:
Reviewed [PR #410](https://github.com/mryfmo/dotfiles/pull/410) at `513e5d0532ec3c21484ab215ef77fea440329749` against the specified base. Found two execution-boundary vulnerabilities and one scope violation.

1. **P1 — High confidence — `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:36`:** Caller-supplied `VERDICT_BIN` and matching `PATH` bypass `env -i`, allowing inherited shell functions and tool-selection variables to execute with the operator’s authority.  
   A read-only probe supplied those variables and an exported `git` function; the function executed. Rebuild the execution environment unconditionally rather than trusting an inherited re-entry marker. The threat table’s “workflow and interpreter or environment inputs” closure is therefore unsupported.

2. **P1 — High confidence — `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:168`:** The dynamic import can execute attacker-supplied bytecode from ignored `scripts/__pycache__`, which the application guard at line 114 excludes from inspection.  
   An in-memory loader probe confirmed that valid cached bytecode executes under `-I -B`; `-B` prevents cache writes, not reads. This permits code execution before the integration gate, despite unchanged tracked helper sources. Load the verified source without consulting checkout caches, including the validator imported by that helper.

3. **P2 — High confidence — `.orchestration/dotfiles-T133-verdict-audit-pool-a01/task.md:1`:** The cumulative diff retains 17 predecessor artifacts outside a03’s `allowed_files`, contradicting the required final inventory in a03 `task.md:70`.  
   The worker report acknowledges these files, but their removal remains unfinished at the reviewed head.

The threat-table assessment is:

| Control row | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Verdict implementation | Execution isolation fails findings 1–2. |
| Verdict schema | Output-validation coverage is present; no additional issue identified. |
| Audit schema | Constraint rejection tests and controlled enum are present. |
| Tests | Fake-tool fixtures cover gate ordering but miss both execution vulnerabilities. |
| a03 artifact directory | Its files remain within the declared directory. |
| Replacement task copy | Replacement is present and a02 copy absent; dispatched-byte authenticity was not independently verified. |
| Branch evidence records | Historical results are labelled; final inventory remains incomplete. |
| Task metadata | External consistency checks remain an acceptance dependency. |
| Metadata separators | Parsing stops at the first closing separator; YAML uses `safe_load`. |
| In-process effects | Dispatch/merge fail closed on gate failure, but findings 1–2 execute earlier. |
| Workflow/interpreter/environment | Claimed closure fails finding 1. |
| New or renamed scripts | Implementation inventory matches; predecessor records violate the overall inventory. |
| Merge effect | Gate precedes dispatch and head-matched merge. |
| Update-branch effect | Fresh evidence is an external orchestration requirement, not verified here. |
| Forced worker removal | No removal implementation is introduced. |

No additional shell interpolation, unsafe YAML construction, or task-ID traversal issue was identified. The changed-file scan found no credential-pattern or home-directory-path matches.

📝 まとめ: Completed the read-only security review; two P1 findings and one P2 finding remain for orchestrator disposition.

Not checked: full tests, live application, or live CI—GitHub access failed. The execution risks above remain unresolved; no files were changed.
Verdict: incorrect
 succeeded in 113ms:
Found four issues in [PR #410](https://github.com/mryfmo/dotfiles/pull/410), at head `6b7e7833a85528440dc397c2555bc9dd603c685a`.

1. **P1 · high confidence · `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:33`** — The cleanup invokes `compgen` before removing inherited functions, so an exported function named `compgen` executes with the operator’s authority before sanitization. A read-only probe confirmed execution.

2. **P1 · high confidence · `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:40`** — A caller-supplied `VERDICT_BIN` containing absolute symlinks, together with the permitted environment and matching `PATH`, passes as trusted without authenticating the targets; a symlink to an attacker’s interpreter is accepted. The regression tests cover a copied executable and an environment forcing reconstruction, but omit this clean-environment/symlink combination.

3. **P2 · high confidence · `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:271`** — Shadow logging follows an unchecked `.orchestration/validation/verdict-shadow.tsv` symlink and appends even when the verdict is `hold`, allowing a PR-controlled link to corrupt an operator-writable file outside the repository. The JSON output redirection at line 268 likewise lacks destination containment checks.

4. **P2 · high confidence · `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh:231`** — Missing, null, or malformed `security` values select “not a security task,” bypassing the security-review requirement instead of holding; isolated probes confirmed missing and null values pass condition 8. Only an explicitly validated boolean `false` should permit this exemption.

The threat-table assessment, using its rows in declaration order:

| Rows / controls | Assessment |
|---|---|
| 1 — verdict implementation; 4 — regression fixtures | Incomplete: the cases above remain uncovered. |
| 2 — verdict schema; 3 — audit schema | No additional defect found; in-memory positive and rejection probes passed. |
| 5 — branch artifacts; 7 — evidence records; 12 — file inventory | Inventory stays within the declared scope; no embedded secrets found. Historical results are identified, but live CI could not be independently verified. |
| 6 — replacement task copy | Its SHA-256 matches the report; the external dispatched original was unavailable for comparison. |
| 8 — metadata; 9 — separators | YAML uses `safe_load` and bounded front matter; metadata validation does not prevent finding 4 in the evaluator. |
| 10 — in-process effects | Gate-before-dispatch/merge ordering holds, but artifact writes have finding 3. |
| 11 — workflow/interpreter/environment inputs | **Not closed:** findings 1 and 2 defeat the claimed execution boundary. |
| 13 — merge | Gate failure prevents dispatch and merge; merge arguments retain the head match. The current gate’s incompatible positional interface normally holds application, as documented. |
| 14 — update-branch; 15 — forced removal | Procedural controls outside this implementation; their execution was not verified. |

Bash syntax, shellcheck, shfmt, and isolated schema probes passed. No additional command-injection, unsafe-deserialization, or embedded-secret issue was identified.

📝 まとめ: Read-only security review completed with four findings; acceptance remains with the orchestrator.
Not run: full tests, Bats, or live application; GitHub access failed. The execution-boundary and file-write risks above remain unresolved.

    76	## Goal
    77	
    78	The orchestrator obtains one schema-valid verdict from nine positively checked acceptance conditions. `--apply` reaches neither acceptance dispatch nor merge without a successful integration gate for the accepted task, head and PR. Later cap amendments govern condition 5.
    79	
    80	The verdict implementation, both JSON schemas and the verdict tests form one independently reviewable PR. The retained interpreter, finding-count, feedback and loop-refresh fixes remain enforced.

    82	## Files
    83	
    84	1. `scripts/orchestrator/verdict.sh <pr> [--apply] [--loop] [--shadow]` remains Bash with `set -Eeuo pipefail`, English shdoc documentation and `jq` for JSON. Every condition is `{id, ok, evidence}` and becomes true only through a positive check of parsed, validated input.
    85	
    86	   Inputs are `gh pr view <pr> --json headRefOid,isDraft,baseRefName,state,number`, `gh pr checks <pr> --json name,state`, the acceptance record whose JSON block names the PR, task and current head and contains a ledger array, its prose `precheck: <sha256>`, the selected task or `--task <id>`, `.orchestration/validation/<task>-audit-<sha7>.json`, a fresh `scripts/pr-feedback.py <pr> --json <mktemp>` sweep, the saved `.orchestration/validation/<task>-pr-feedback.json` dispositions, the task's `allowed_files`, `security` and Check caps, regime counters, the merge-base diff and numstat, and the required head-specific security review. The PR is open, non-draft and based on `main`. A missing head is fetched from `pull/<pr>/head` and must resolve locally before evaluation continues.
    87	
    88	   Condition 1 verifies that `git show <head>:.orchestration/<task>/precheck.json` hashes to the record's `precheck:` value and names the head or an ancestor whose diff to the head contains only `.orchestration/<task>/` paths. Unset or zero `PRECHECK_REQUIRED` makes failure of this condition a warning.
    89	
    90	   Condition 2 requires every required check to be present with state `SUCCESS` for the head. Empty or unreadable checks hold. Every duplicate entry for a required name must also be `SUCCESS`.
    91	
    92	   Condition 3 requires an audit that validates against `.schemas/audit.json`, names this task and head, has zero open P0/P1 findings, and has verdict `correct` or a non-empty set of remaining findings whose scopes are all `records`. A blocked audit holds. An incorrect audit without remaining findings fails this condition.
    93	
    94	   Condition 4 requires both saved and fresh feedback documents to name the accepted head, complete feedback identity matching with multiplicity, and a valid disposition for every fresh item. Diagnostic URLs do not determine the result.
    95	
    96	   Condition 5 requires every path in `git diff --name-only <base> <head>` to match an `allowed_files` entry and additions-plus-deletions totals from `git diff --numstat --no-renames <base> <head>` to remain within the effective Check caps. The base is `git merge-base origin/main <head>`. An unreadable base or diff holds. Backticked exclusions after `outside` leave the outside count. Default exclusions are `tests/` and `.orchestration/`. Item 7 defines amendment precedence.
    97	
    98	   Condition 6 runs `scripts/regime-counters.sh <task> --pr <pr>` with its ledger source. No `S1:` line is present and the parsed `revises` value is below 2. A missing ledger, failed command or unreadable count holds.
    99	
   100	   Condition 7 requires the numeric count of open P0/P1 findings to be zero. Findings with status `new`, `still-open` or absent count as open regardless of identifier or scope.

   103

   105	
   106	   All nine conditions passing produces `accept`. Failure of condition 6 or 7, missing or unreadable input, schema-invalid input or an internal evaluation error produces `hold`. Other failed conditions produce `revise`. The revise line remains `AGMSG-ACCEPTANCE v1 task_id=<task> status=revise previous_head=<head> reason=<failed ids> next_action=<the first failed condition's evidence, shell-safe>`. The orchestrator supplies `check=` selectors.
   107	
   108	   The output is `.orchestration/validation/<task>-verdict-<sha7>.json`, validated against `.schemas/verdict.json`, with `verdict`, `pr`, `head`, `task_id`, `conditions[]`, `inputs_sha256{}`, `generated_at` and optional `revise_line`. Exit codes remain 0 for accept, 10 for revise, 20 for hold and 2 for usage or internal error, with an internal error treated as hold. Refusal before a usable task or head exists reports hold without inventing an output identity.
   109	
   110	   `--apply` requires the pinned execution context, tracked scripts, schemas and task files equal to `origin/main`, and no untracked executable helper substituted in those code directories. The last four rows of `.orchestration/validation/verdict-shadow.tsv` must agree between machine and human and represent four distinct PRs. Four rows for one PR do not unlock application. Item 6 adds the integration gate before either application effect.
   111	
   112	   After accept and a successful gate, application writes `AGMSG-ACCEPTANCE v1 task_id=<task> status=accepted reason=verdict-<sha7> next_action=merge` to a temporary file, sends it through `scripts/orchestrator/dispatch.sh <team> <from> <to> <pane> <file> <record>`, and invokes `gh pr merge <pr> --squash --match-head-commit <head>`. Team, sender, recipient and placement come from `--team`, `--from`, `--to` and `--pane`. Arguments containing `--admin` or `--auto` anywhere are refused with exit 2 before any read or tool invocation.
   113	
   114	   `--shadow` writes the verdict JSON and appends `<pr>\t<head>\t<verdict>\t\t\t<generated_at>` to `.orchestration/validation/verdict-shadow.tsv`, with columns `pr`, `head`, `machine`, `human`, `agree` and `at`. The orchestrator supplies the human and agreement columns. Shadow mode sends nothing and merges nothing.
   115	
   116	   `--loop` processes eligible open, non-draft PRs on `main` in PR-number order when an acceptance record exists and names the PR's current head. It retains the pinned context, fetches origin between PRs, and with `--apply` fast-forwards the trusted checkout to `origin/main` after each child. Failure to fast-forward holds with exit 20. Fetching alone does not satisfy the retained refresh requirement.

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Collect every piece of GitHub feedback on a pull request head into one JSON document.
     3	
     4	Usage: pr-feedback.py <pr-number> [--repo owner/name] [--json <out>]
     5	
     6	Items cover issue comments, reviews, inline review comments (with their
     7	thread's resolution state), non-passing check runs, every check-run
     8	annotation at any level, and every commit status on the PR head. Each item
     9	carries an empty `disposition` to fill with `fixed:<commit>` or
    10	`not-applicable:<reason>` before integration; scripts/require-crit-review.py
    11	checks the filled file through PR_FEEDBACK_EVIDENCE. Passing check runs are
    12	listed under `checks` only.
    13	"""
    14	
    15	from __future__ import annotations
    16	
    17	import argparse
    18	import datetime
    19	import json
    20	import os
    21	import subprocess
    22	import sys
    23	from collections import Counter
    24	from collections.abc import Callable
    25	from pathlib import Path
    26	from typing import Any
    27	
    28	PASSING_CONCLUSIONS = {"success", "neutral", "skipped"}
    29	THREADS_QUERY = """
    30	query($owner: String!, $name: String!, $number: Int!, $cursor: String) {
    31	  repository(owner: $owner, name: $name) {
    32	    pullRequest(number: $number) {
    33	      reviewThreads(first: 100, after: $cursor) {
    34	        pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    35	        nodes {
    36	          id
    37	          isResolved
    38	          isOutdated
    39	          comments(first: 100) {
    40	            pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    41	            nodes { databaseId }
    42	          }
    43	        }
    44	      }
    45	    }
    46	  }
    47	}
    48	"""
    49	THREAD_COMMENTS_QUERY = """
    50	query($id: ID!, $cursor: String) {
    51	  node(id: $id) {
    52	    ... on PullRequestReviewThread {
    53	      comments(first: 100, after: $cursor) {
    54	        pageInfo { hasNextPage endCursor }
    55	        nodes { databaseId }
    56	      }
    57	    }
    58	  }
    59	}
    60	"""
    61	
    62	Fetch = Callable[[str, bool], Any]
    63	GraphQL = Callable[[str, dict[str, Any]], Any]
    64	
    65	
    66	def gh_env() -> dict[str, str]:
    67	    """Environment for gh that never colours output, even under CLICOLOR_FORCE panes."""
    68	    env = {key: value for key, value in os.environ.items() if key not in {"CLICOLOR_FORCE", "GH_FORCE_TTY"}}
    69	    env["NO_COLOR"] = "1"
    70	    return env
    71	
    72	
    73	def gh(args: list[str]) -> str:
    74	    result = subprocess.run(["gh", *args], capture_output=True, text=True, check=False, env=gh_env())
    75	    if result.returncode != 0:
    76	        sys.exit(f"gh {' '.join(args[:2])} failed: {result.stderr.strip()}")
    77	    return result.stdout
    78	
    79	
    80	def gh_fetch(path: str, paginate: bool = False) -> Any:
    81	    """Return the JSON for a REST path; paginated responses become a list of pages."""
    82	    if paginate:
    83	        return json.loads(gh(["api", "--paginate", "--slurp", path]))
    84	    return json.loads(gh(["api", path]))
    85	
    86	
    87	def gh_graphql(query: str, variables: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:
    88	    args = ["api", "graphql", "-f", f"query={query}"]
    89	    for key, value in variables.items():
    90	        if value is not None:
    91	            args.extend(["-F" if type(value) is int else "-f", f"{key}={value}"])
    92	    return json.loads(gh(args))
    93	
    94

   108	def flatten(pages: Any, key: str | None = None) -> list[Any]:
   109	    """Merge `gh api --paginate --slurp` pages into one list."""
   110	    merged: list[Any] = []
   111	    for page in pages:
   112	        merged.extend(page[key] if key else page)

   114

   117	    if not actor:

328:9. `.claude/settings.json` retains its tracked project hooks and rewrites every command through the installed orchestrator-only guard.
330:   This tracked file is not a chezmoi template and remains portable across hosts. Every command starts with `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME="$HOME" LANG=C.UTF-8 TERM=dumb CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}" CLAUDE_ENV_FILE="${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE}"` followed by `"$HOME/.claude/hooks/orchestrator-only.sh"` and a fixed operation identifier with its arguments. The shell running the hook expands `$HOME`, and the clean environment re-exports the launcher's HOME before any path that uses it. The file names no interpreter, anchor path, private links directory or host-specific tool directory.
340:    Every Claude worker pane starts with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<absolute-home>/.claude-worker`. This value reaches the actual worker process through the pane command and environment construction. Setting it only in the parent spawn process does not satisfy this requirement.
342:    Managed-workspace and standalone-worker routes receive the same binding. Repeated starts do not inherit a conflicting caller Claude configuration directory. This task introduces no worker gh configuration directory, gh templates or credential-separation claim.
346:    The launcher stops writing Claude worker delivery hooks and merge denials into worktree-local `.claude/settings.local.json`. It renders the worker delivery hooks and native merge denials into `~/.claude-worker/settings.json`, the user-level settings file selected for that directory.
352:    The launcher also regenerates its managed hooks in the main checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json`. Every local agmsg delivery and orchestrator attachment command that it writes runs through the installed orchestrator-only guard. The existing permissions object and unrelated non-hook settings remain stored. Their effective sandbox interpretation follows the administrative managed policy.
